People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I mean… nobody told me I had anything like that before.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, has your ability to do your job gone downhill?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has. I’ve made some mistakes at the shop, and they took me off the high-voltage EV stuff because they were worried I wasn’t being safe. And… things at home got bad too. My husband ended up taking me in for an emergency evaluation.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling emotionally flat or not caring much about things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I just kind of stop answering people. I don’t go to the church potlucks anymore, and I used to help with those. Some days I just sit at home, or at work staring at the tools, and I can’t make myself care.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know. I’ve had bad stretches, but I can’t say for sure it was most of the day every day for two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you heard the voice or felt people were tracking you even though you were not feeling unusually high, energized, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a stretch like that. I wasn’t all revved up or making big plans, and I wasn’t… really depressed either. I was sleeping more, things were quieter. But I still heard that voice in the garage, and I still thought my husband and my boss were tracking me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. There were times I’d go for weeks only sleeping maybe two or three hours. I’d wake up before dawn already thinking about the garage, and it felt like going back to sleep would just be wasting time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean… nobody told me I had anything like that before.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, has your ability to do your job gone downhill?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has. I’ve made some mistakes at the shop, and they took me off the high-voltage EV stuff because they were worried I wasn’t being safe. And… things at home got bad too. My husband ended up taking me in for an emergency evaluation.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling emotionally flat or not caring much about things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I just kind of stop answering people. I don’t go to the church potlucks anymore, and I used to help with those. Some days I just sit at home, or at work staring at the tools, and I can’t make myself care.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know. I’ve had bad stretches, but I can’t say for sure it was most of the day every day for two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you heard the voice or felt people were tracking you even though you were not feeling unusually high, energized, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a stretch like that. I wasn’t all revved up or making big plans, and I wasn’t… really depressed either. I was sleeping more, things were quieter. But I still heard that voice in the garage, and I still thought my husband and my boss were tracking me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. There were times I’d go for weeks only sleeping maybe two or three hours. I’d wake up before dawn already thinking about the garage, and it felt like going back to sleep would just be wasting time.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you felt full of energy, were you more active than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I’d stay after closing at the shop, start a few repair jobs at once, mess with the parts shelves, make plans for car stuff… and still feel like I needed to keep moving.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy times, did you feel like you had special abilities or powers beyond what other people have?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I didn’t think I had powers exactly. But I was pretty sure I saw things other people didn’t. Like I was the only one who really understood where the repair business was going, and if people would just listen to me, I could make our little shop the top EV place in the state.\n\nDoctor: During one of those high-energy times, were you hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that. I wasn’t… I mean, I don’t think I was hospitalized just for that.\n\nDoctor: During one of those high-energy times, were you admitted to a hospital because of it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… I don’t think I was admitted because of feeling energized, not exactly. It’s kind of blurry.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. My husband ended up taking me in for an emergency evaluation.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling emotionally flat or not caring much about things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Some days I just sit at home, or at work staring at the tools, and I can’t make myself care.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know. Some days I just sit at home, or at work staring at the tools, and I can’t make myself care.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know. I’ve had bad stretches, but I can’t say for sure it was most of the day every day for two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you heard the voice or felt people were tracking you even though you were not feeling unusually high, energized, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a stretch like that. I wasn’t all revved up or making big plans, and I wasn’t… really depressed either. I was sleeping more, things were quieter. But I still heard that voice in the garage, and I still thought my husband and my boss were tracking me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. There were times I’d go for weeks only sleeping maybe two or three hours. I’d wake up before dawn already thinking about the garage, and it felt like going back to sleep would just be wasting time.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you felt full of energy, were you more active than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I’d stay after closing at the shop, start a few repair jobs at once, mess with the parts shelves, make plans for car stuff… and still feel like I needed to keep moving.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy times, did you feel like you had special abilities or powers beyond what other people have?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I didn’t think I had powers exactly. But I was pretty sure I saw things other people didn’t. Like I was the only one who really understood where the repair business was going, and if people would just listen to me, I could make our little shop the top EV place in the state.\n\nDoctor: During one of those high-energy times, were you hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that. I wasn’t… I mean, I don’t think I was hospitalized just for that.\n\nDoctor: During one of those high-energy times, were you admitted to a hospital because of it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… I don’t think I was admitted because of feeling energized, not exactly. It’s kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: During one of those high-energy times, were you ever admitted to a hospital because of your behavior?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know. People were upset with how I was acting, but whether I was admitted because of that specifically… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Did those high-energy periods ever last at least 7 days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more than seven days. It’s been going on, in stretches, for… I don’t know, maybe around eleven months. Long time.\n\nDoctor: Over the past ten months, have those high-energy periods taken up most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most of the last year, it’s felt like that—talking too fast, taking on too much, just… driven, like I can’t slow down.\n\nDoctor: Have these problems been going on for at least 6 months?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s been more than six months… probably about a year and a few months. Since things at home got worse over me being out late and taking on stuff at the shop.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard the voice or felt people were tracking you during a time when you were not feeling unusually high or energized?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there were quieter weeks, when I was sleeping more and not making big plans. But I still heard that voice at the garage, and I still thought my husband and my boss were… tracking me somehow.\n\nDoctor: During the time since these problems began, have the high-energy periods been present for most of the illness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of it, I think. This past year has mostly been those stretches where I’m talking too fast, taking on too much, feeling like I can’t slow down."
